# PENAL CODE

PENAL CODE

(NOTE 1: See the law of 10 July 1996 abolishing the death penalty and modifying criminal penalties, in particular article 3, 1996-07-10/42)

(NOTE: Consultation of previous versions from 05-04-1990 and update to 29-

## BOOK 1. - OFFENCES AND PUNISHMENT IN GENERAL.

##### CHAPTER I. - OFFENCES.

###### Article 1. An offence that the laws punish with a criminal penalty is a crime.

An offence that the laws punish with a correctional penalty is a misdemeanour. An offence that the laws punish with a police penalty is a petty offence.

###### Art. 2. No offence may be punished with penalties that were not provided by

law before the offence was committed.

If the penalty established at the time of judgment differs from that which was provided at the time of the offence, the lesser penalty shall be applied.

###### Art. 3. An offence committed on the territory of the Kingdom, by Belgians or by

foreigners, is punished in accordance with the provisions of Belgian laws.

###### Art. 4. An offence committed outside the territory of the Kingdom, by Belgians or by

foreigners, is punished, in Belgium, only in the cases determined by law.

###### Art. 5.[1 Any legal person is criminally liable for offences that

are intrinsically linked to the realisation of its object or to the defence of its interests, or for those whose concrete facts demonstrate that they were committed on its behalf.

The following are assimilated to legal persons:

1° temporary partnerships and internal partnerships;

2° the companies referred to in article 2, § 4, paragraph 2, of the Companies Code, as well as commercial companies in formation;

3° civil companies that have not taken the form of a commercial company. The criminal liability of legal persons does not exclude that of natural persons who are authors of the same acts or who participated therein.]1

(1)<L 2018-07-11/10, art. 2, 132; In force: 30-07-2018>

###### Art. 6. The courts and tribunals shall continue to apply the particular laws and regulations

in all matters not regulated by this Code.

##### CHAPTER II. - PENALTIES.

###### SECTION I. - THE VARIOUS TYPES OF PENALTIES.

###### Art. 7. The penalties applicable to offences (committed by natural

persons) are: <L 1999-05-04/60, art. 3, 024; In force: 02-07-1999>

In criminal matters:

(1° imprisonment for serious offences;

2° detention.) <L 1996-07-10/42, art. 4, 018; In force: 11-08-1996>

[2 In correctional and police matters:

1° imprisonment;

2° electronic monitoring penalty;

3° community service penalty;

4° autonomous probation penalty.

The penalties provided for in 1° to 4° may not be applied cumulatively.]2

In criminal and correctional matters:

1° Deprivation of certain political and civil rights;

2° [1 placement at the disposal of the sentence implementation court;]1

In criminal, correctional and police matters:

1° Fine;

2° Special confiscation.

(1)<L 2007-04-26/89, art. 2, 069; In force: 01-01-2012>

05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. 13), modified in the same sense by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 48, 114>

1° fine;

2° special confiscation; the special confiscation provided for in article 42, 1°, pronounced with regard to legal persons under public law, may only concern civilly seizable property; in criminal and correctional matters:

1° dissolution; this may not be pronounced with regard to legal persons under public law;

2° prohibition from exercising an activity falling within the corporate object, with the exception of activities that fall within a public service mission;

3° closure of one or more establishments, with the exception of establishments where activities that fall within a public service mission are carried out; 4° publication or dissemination of the decision.

###### SECTION II. - CRIMINAL PENALTIES.

###### Art. 8.

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 5, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Imprisonment for serious offences is for life or for a term.

###### Art. 9.

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 6, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Imprisonment for serious offences for a term is pronounced for a period of:

1° five to ten years;

2° ten to fifteen years;

3° fifteen to twenty years;

4° twenty to thirty years.

[1 5° thirty to forty years.]1

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 2, 114; In force: 29-02-2016>

###### Art. 10.

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 7, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Detention is for life or for a term.

###### Art. 11.

<L 1996-07-10/42, art. 8, 018; In force: 11-08-1996> Detention for a term is pronounced for a period of:

1° five to ten years;

2° ten to fifteen years;

3° fifteen to twenty years;

4° twenty to thirty years.

[1 5° thirty to forty years.]1

###### SECTION III. - CORRECTIONAL IMPRISONMENT.

###### Art. 25.[1 The duration of correctional imprisonment is, except in cases provided by

law, at least eight days and at most five years.

It is at most five years if it concerns a crime punishable by imprisonment of five to ten years that has been correctionalized.

It is at most ten years if it concerns a crime punishable by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years that has been correctionalized.

It is at most fifteen years if it concerns a crime punishable by imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years that has been correctionalized.

[2 It is at most twenty-eight years if it concerns a crime punishable by imprisonment of twenty to thirty years that has been correctionalized.

It is at most thirty-eight years if it concerns a crime punishable by imprisonment of thirty to forty years that has been correctionalized.

It is at most forty years if it concerns a crime punishable by life imprisonment that has been correctionalized.]2]1

The duration of one day of imprisonment is twenty-four hours.

The duration of one month of imprisonment is thirty days.

(1)<L 2009-12-21/14, art. 2, 075; En vigueur : 21-01-2010>

###### Subsection II. - (Penalties common to crimes and offenses applicable to

legal persons). <Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6; In force: 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 35.

<L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> Dissolution may be decided by the judge when the legal person was intentionally created in order to exercise the punishable activities for which it is convicted or when its purpose was intentionally diverted in order to exercise such activities.

When ordering dissolution, the judge refers the case to the competent jurisdiction to hear the liquidation of the legal person.

###### Art. 36.

<L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> The temporary or permanent prohibition from exercising an activity falling within the corporate purpose of the legal person may be pronounced by the judge in cases provided for by law.

###### Art. 37.

<L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6, 024; In force: 02-07-1999> The temporary or permanent closure of one or more establishments of the legal person may be pronounced by the judge in cases provided for by law.

Art. 37bis. <Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art. 6; In force: 02-07-1999> The publication or dissemination of the decision at the expense of the convicted person may be pronounced by the judge in cases determined by law.

SECTION Vbis. ([2 former section Vter renumbered as new section Vbis]2) [1 - Electronic monitoring sentence]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-02-07/15, art. 6, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016>

05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. Art. 37ter.[1 § 1. When an act is of a nature to result in a term of imprisonment of one year at most, the judge may sentence as a principal penalty to an electronic monitoring sentence of a duration equal to the term of imprisonment that he would have pronounced and which may be applicable in case of non-execution of the electronic monitoring sentence. For the determination of the duration of this subsidiary imprisonment sentence, one day of the electronic monitoring sentence imposed corresponds to one day of imprisonment sentence.

An electronic monitoring sentence consists of the obligation to be present at a specified address, except for authorized movements or absences, during a period determined by the judge in accordance with paragraph 2. The control of presence is ensured in particular by the use of electronic means and, in accordance with paragraph 5, this obligation is accompanied by conditions.

The electronic monitoring sentence may not be pronounced for acts: 1° referred to in articles 375 to 377;

2° referred to in articles 379 to 387, if the acts were committed on minors or with the assistance of minors;

3° referred to in articles 393 to 397.

###### § 2. The duration of the electronic monitoring sentence may not be less than

one month nor more than one year. In accordance with article 85, the criminal judge may take into account mitigating circumstances without however the fixed duration of electronic monitoring as an autonomous sentence being able to be less than one month.

The electronic monitoring sentence [2 must begin]2 within six months following the date on which the judicial decision became final. If the exceeding of this period is attributable to the convicted person, the public prosecutor decides either to postpone the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence again, or to proceed with the execution of the subsidiary imprisonment. If the exceeding of this period is not attributable to the convicted person, the sentence must be executed within six months following the expiration of the first period, failing which it is time-barred.

###### § 3. For the purpose of applying an electronic monitoring sentence, the

public prosecutor, the examining magistrate, the investigating courts or the trial courts may instruct the competent service for the organization and control of electronic monitoring, hereinafter referred to as "competent service for electronic monitoring", of the judicial district of the place of residence of the accused, the defendant or the convicted person to prepare a brief information report and/or a social investigation.

This report or investigation contains only the relevant elements likely to inform the authority that addressed the request to the competent service for electronic monitoring on the appropriateness of the envisaged sentence.

Any adult person with whom the defendant cohabits is heard in their observations within the framework of this social investigation. The brief information report or the social investigation report is attached to the file within one month of the request.

###### § 4. When an electronic monitoring sentence is contemplated by the judge,

requested by the public prosecutor or sought by the defendant, the judge informs the latter, before closing the proceedings, of the scope of such a sentence, provides any indications as to the concrete content it may have and as to the individualized conditions that may be imposed in accordance with paragraph 5, and hears them in their observations. The judge may also take into account, in this regard, the interests of any victims. The judge may only pronounce the electronic monitoring sentence if the defendant is present or represented at the hearing and after they have given their consent, either in person or through their counsel. Any adult cohabitant of the defendant who has not been heard within the framework of the social investigation, or if no social investigation has been carried out, may be heard by the judge in their observations.

The judge who refuses to pronounce an electronic monitoring sentence requested by the public prosecutor or sought by the defendant must justify their decision.

###### § 5. The judge determines the duration of the electronic monitoring sentence and may

provide indications as to its concrete modalities.

The electronic monitoring sentence is always subject to the following general conditions:

1° not to commit offences;

2° to have a fixed address and, in case of change of address, to communicate without delay the address of their new residence to the public prosecutor and to the competent service for electronic monitoring;

3° to respond to summonses from the competent service for electronic monitoring and to comply with the concrete modalities determined by this service. The judge may further subject the convicted person to individualized special conditions in the interest of victims. These conditions relate to the prohibition of frequenting certain places or contacting the victim and/or to compensating them.]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2014-02-07/15, art. 7, 110; En vigueur : indéterminée et au plus tard au 01-05-2016 (voir L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, modifié lui-même par L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13), art. 7 réécrit par L 2016-02-05/11, art. 44>

(2)<L 2018-07-11/02, art. 7, 131; En vigueur : 28-07-2018>

Art. 37quater. [1 § 1er. As soon as the conviction to an electronic monitoring sentence has become final, the clerk informs the competent service for electronic monitoring with a view to executing this sentence. For this purpose, this service contacts the convicted person within seven working days following the notification, determines the concrete modalities of execution of the sentence, after having heard the convicted person and taking into account their observations, and notifies the competent public prosecutor without delay.

###### § 2. Without prejudice to the application of article 20 of the law of 5 August 1992 on the

police function, the public prosecutor is responsible for the supervision of the convicted person. The officials of the competent service for electronic monitoring supervise the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence and ensure the follow-up or guidance of the convicted person.

###### § 3. If the electronic monitoring sentence is not executed in whole or in

part in accordance with the provisions of article 37ter, § 5, the official of the competent service for electronic monitoring informs the competent public prosecutor without delay. The latter may then decide to proceed with the execution of the imprisonment sentence fixed in the judicial decision, taking into account the part of the electronic monitoring sentence that has already been executed by the convicted person. In this case, one day of electronic monitoring sentence executed is equivalent to one day of imprisonment. If the total or partial non-execution concerns new offences, it must be established by a decision that has become final that the convicted person committed a misdemeanour or a crime, or an equivalent offence taken into account in accordance with article 99bis, during the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence.

The competent public prosecutor justifies their decision and communicates it by means of the fastest written means of communication:

- to the convicted person;
- to the chief of the local police of the municipality where the convicted person resides;
- to the national database referred to in article 44/2 of the law of 5 August 1992 on the police function;
- to the competent service for electronic monitoring.

###### § 4. If the electronic monitoring sentence reaches or exceeds three months, the

convicted person may request a suspension of the control carried out by electronic means after having served one third of the duration of the sentence. He is informed of this possibility by the service competent for electronic monitoring as soon as the sentence is put into execution. As soon as he meets the time conditions, the convicted person may address a written request to the competent public prosecutor's office with a view to obtaining this suspension. The convicted person sends a copy of this written request to the service competent for electronic monitoring.

Within fifteen days, the service competent for electronic monitoring renders an opinion to the competent public prosecutor's office regarding compliance with the programme of the concrete content of electronic monitoring and, where applicable, the individualised special conditions imposed on the convicted person. This opinion indicates whether the convicted person has committed new offences during the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence. In addition, it includes a reasoned proposal to grant or reject the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means and sets out, where applicable, the special conditions that the service competent for electronic monitoring deems necessary to impose on the convicted person.

The competent public prosecutor's office grants within one month after receipt of the opinion the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means in the case where the convicted person has not committed new offences and has complied with the programme of the concrete content of electronic monitoring and, where applicable, the individualised special conditions that had been imposed on him. When the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means is granted, the convicted person is subject to a probation period for the part of the electronic monitoring sentence that he still has to serve. In this case, one day of the probation period is equivalent to one day of the electronic monitoring sentence imposed. The convicted person is subject to the general conditions, as well as, where applicable, to the special conditions that have been imposed on him.

The competent public prosecutor's office communicates its decision by the fastest written means of communication:

- to the convicted person;
- to the chief of the local police force of the municipality where the convicted person resides;
- to the national database referred to in Article 44/2 of the Act of 5 August 1992 on the police function;
- to the service competent for electronic monitoring.

In case of rejection of a request for suspension, a new request may only be submitted after the expiry of a period of two months from the rejection. In case of non-compliance with the general conditions and, where applicable, the special conditions imposed on the convicted person, the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means may be revoked.

The competent public prosecutor's office hears the convicted person in his observations on the matter. If the convicted person does not respond to the summons for the purpose of being heard, this public prosecutor's office may decide to revoke the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means or to proceed with the execution of the subsidiary imprisonment. If the non-compliance concerns the general condition prohibiting the commission of new offences, it must be established by a decision that has acquired the force of res judicata that the convicted person has committed a misdemeanour or a felony, or an equivalent offence taken into account in accordance with Article 99bis, during the execution of the electronic monitoring sentence or during the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means.

The decision to revoke the suspension of the control carried out by electronic means includes a decision on:

- the special conditions linked to the suspension, imposed by the public prosecutor's office;
- the execution of electronic monitoring for the remaining duration of the probation period;
- the reinstatement of the special conditions imposed where applicable by the trial court.

The competent public prosecutor's office communicates its decision by the fastest written means of communication:

- to the convicted person;
- to the chief of the local police force of the municipality where the convicted person resides;
- to the national database referred to in Article 44/2 of the Act of 5 August 1992 on the police function;
- to the service competent for electronic monitoring.

###### § 5. The public prosecutor's office referred to in paragraphs 1 to 4 is the public prosecutor's office at

the trial court that has pronounced the conviction to a sentence under electronic monitoring.]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-02-07/15, art. 8, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13), art. 8, rewritten by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 45>

SECTION Vter. - ([1 former section Vbis renumbered as new section Vter]1) ON THE COMMUNITY SERVICE SENTENCE <Inserted by L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; In force (1)<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01- Art. 37quinquies.([1 former art. 37ter renumbered as new art. 37quinquies]1) <Inserted by L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; In force: 07-05-2002> § 1. When an act is of a nature to entail a police sentence or a correctional sentence, the judge may impose as a principal sentence a community service sentence. The judge provides, within the limits of the sentences provided for the offence and by the law according to his jurisdiction, an imprisonment sentence or a fine that may be applicable in case of non-execution of the community service sentence.

[3 The community service sentence may not be imposed for acts:

1° which would be punishable, if they were not transmuted into misdemeanours, by a maximum sentence exceeding twenty years of imprisonment;

2° referred to in Articles 375 to 377;

3° referred to in Articles 379 to 387, if the acts were committed on minors or with the assistance of minors;

4° referred to in Articles 393 to 397.]3

###### § 2. The duration of a community service sentence may not be less than twenty hours nor

exceed three hundred hours. A community service sentence equal to or less than forty-five hours constitutes a police sentence. A community service sentence of more than forty-five hours constitutes a correctional sentence.

The community service sentence must be executed within twelve months following the date on which the judicial decision has acquired the force of res judicata. The probation commission may ex officio or at the request of the convicted person extend this period.

###### § 3. When a community service sentence is being considered by the judge, requested by the

public prosecutor's office or sought by the accused, the judge informs the latter, before the close of the proceedings, of the scope of such a sentence and hears him in his observations. The judge may also take into account, in this regard, the interests of possible victims. The judge may only impose the community service sentence if the accused is present or represented at the hearing and after he has given, either in person or through his counsel, his consent.

[2 The judge who refuses to impose a community service sentence requested by the public prosecutor's office or sought by the accused, must give reasons for his decision.]2

###### § 4. The judge determines the duration of the work sentence and may give

indications concerning the concrete content of the work sentence.

(1)<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01- 05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. (3)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 12, 114; In force: 29-02-2016>

Art. 37sexies.([1 former art. 37quater renumbered as new art. 37sexies]1) <Inserted by L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; In force: 07-05-2002> § 1er. The work sentence is performed free of charge by the convicted person during the time left free by his or her possible school or professional activities.

The work sentence may only be performed at public services of the State, municipalities, provinces, communities and regions or at non-profit associations or foundations with social, scientific or cultural purposes.

The work sentence may not consist of work that, in the designated public service or association, is generally performed by paid workers.

###### § 2. For the application of article 37ter, the public prosecutor, the investigating

judge, the investigating courts and the trial courts may instruct the section of the Probation Service of (FPS Justice) of the judicial district of the place of residence of the accused, the defendant or the convicted person to prepare a brief information report and/or a social investigation. <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 1°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007> (The King specifies the rules relating to the brief information report and the social investigation.

These reports and investigations may only contain relevant elements capable of informing the authority that addressed the request to the probation service about the appropriateness of the measure or sentence under consideration.) <L 2006-12-27/33,

###### art. 35

, 2°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007>

###### § 3. (Each district section of the Probation Service of the FPS

Justice prepares twice a year a report on existing activities suitable for the performance of the work sentence.) The section delivers a copy of this report to the president of the court of first instance and to the public prosecutor of the relevant district and, upon simple request, to any person able to justify an interest. <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 3°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007>

###### (§ 4. At the federal and local levels, consultation structures relating to

the application of the [2 work sentence and the autonomous probation sentence]2 are created. These consultation structures have the mission to bring together on a regular basis the bodies concerned by the execution of the [2 work sentence and the autonomous probation sentence]2 in order to evaluate their collaboration. The King determines the terms of composition and operation of these consultation structures.) <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 35, 4°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007> (1)<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; In force: undetermined and at the latest on 01- 05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02, art. Art. 37septies.([1 former art. 37quinquies renumbered as new art. 37septies)]1) <Inserted by L 2002-04-17/33, art. 3; In force: 07-05-2002> § 1er. The convicted person to whom a work sentence has been imposed pursuant to article 37ter is monitored by a probation officer of the Probation Service of (FPS Justice) of the judicial district of the place of residence of the convicted person. <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 1°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007>

The execution of the work sentence is supervised by the probation commission of the place of residence of the convicted person to which the probation officer reports.

###### § 2. When the judicial decision imposing a work sentence has become

final, the clerk transmits within twenty-four hours a certified copy to the president of the competent probation commission as well as to (the competent district section of the Probation Service of FPS Justice), which immediately designates the probation officer referred to in § 1er. [2 ...]2 (The territorial jurisdiction of the probation commission is determined by the place of residence of the convicted person at the time when the judgment or decision becomes final.

When the person concerned resides outside the territory of the Kingdom, the territorially competent probation commission is that of the place where the conviction was pronounced at first instance.

When, in exceptional cases, the commission considers it appropriate, for a person convicted to a work sentence who makes a reasoned request to that effect, to transfer jurisdiction to the commission of the place of his or her new residence, it takes a reasoned decision, after that other commission has given a favorable opinion within a period of two months. For a person without residence in the Kingdom, jurisdiction may be transferred according to the same procedure to another probation commission, without it being required in that case that it be the commission of the place of his or her new residence.) <L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 4°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007>

###### § 3. After having heard the convicted person and taken into account his or her observations,

the probation officer determines the concrete content of the sentence, in accordance with the indications referred to in article 37ter, § 4, under the supervision of the probation commission which, of its own motion, upon requisition of the public prosecutor or at the request of the convicted person, may at any time, and also in accordance with the indications referred to in article 37ter , § 4, specify and adapt it.

(The concrete content of the work sentence is notified in an agreement to be signed by the convicted person, of which the probation officer gives him or her a copy. The probation officer also communicates a copy of the signed agreement to the probation commission, within a period of three working days.) <L 2006-12- 27/33, art. 36, 5°, 061; In force: 07-01-2007>

###### § 4. In case of total or partial non-performance of the work sentence, the justice

assistant informs the probation commission without delay. The commission summons the convicted person by registered mail [2 or by an electronic means to be defined by the King]2 more than ten days before the date set for the examination of the case and informs his counsel. The commission's file is made available to the convicted person and his counsel for five days.

The commission, sitting outside the presence of the public prosecutor, drafts a [2 reasoned]2 report with a view to the application of the alternative sentence.

(The report is sent by simple letter to the convicted person, to the public prosecutor.) and to the justice assistant.) < L 2006-12-27/33, art. 36, 6°, 061; In force: 07-01- In this case, the public prosecutor may decide to execute the prison sentence or the fine provided for in the judicial decision, taking into account the work sentence that has already been performed by the convicted person. (1)<L 2014-02-07/15, art. 9, 110; In force: indeterminate and at the latest on 01- 05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11-23/02, art. SECTION Vquater. [1 - The autonomous probation sentence]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-10/80, art. 7, 111; In force: indeterminate and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13)>

Art. 37octies.[1 § 1er. Where an act is of a nature to entail a police sentence or a correctional sentence, the judge may sentence, as a principal sentence, to an autonomous probation sentence.

An autonomous probation sentence consists of the obligation to comply with particular conditions during a specified period, set by the judge in accordance with § 2.

The judge provides, within the limits of the sentences provided for the offence and by law according to his jurisdiction, a prison sentence or a fine that may be applicable in case of non-performance of the autonomous probation sentence. The autonomous probation sentence may not be pronounced for acts:

1° which would be punishable, if they were not transmuted into offences, by a maximum sentence exceeding twenty years of imprisonment;

2° referred to in articles 375 to 377;

3° referred to in articles 379 to 387, if the acts were committed on minors or with the help of minors;

4° referred to in articles 393 to 397.

###### § 2. The duration of the autonomous probation sentence may not be less than six

months nor more than two years. An autonomous probation sentence of twelve months or less than twelve months constitutes a police sentence. An autonomous probation sentence of one year or more than one year constitutes a correctional sentence.

###### § 3. Where an autonomous probation sentence is considered by the judge, requested

by the public prosecutor or requested by the accused, the judge informs the latter, before the closure of the proceedings, of the scope of such a sentence and hears him in his observations. The judge may also take into account, in this regard, the interests of possible victims. The judge may only pronounce the autonomous probation sentence if the accused is present or represented at the hearing and after he has given, either in person or through his counsel, his consent. The judge who refuses to pronounce an autonomous probation sentence requested by the public prosecutor or requested by the accused must justify his decision.

###### § 4. The judge determines the duration of the autonomous probation sentence and gives

indications concerning the content of the autonomous probation sentence.

###### § 5. At the federal and local levels, the consultation structures relating to

the application of the work sentence and the autonomous probation sentence function in accordance with the provisions of article 37sexies, § 4.]1 (1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-10/80, art. 8, 111; In force: indeterminate and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13), art. 8 amended by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 51, 114>

Art. 37novies.[1 § 1er. Anyone who has been sentenced to an autonomous probation sentence in accordance with article 37octies shall be subject to judicial guidance exercised by a justice assistant of the Service of the Houses of Justice of the Federal Public Service Justice of the judicial district of the place of his residence.

The execution of the autonomous probation sentence is controlled by the probation commission of the place of residence of the convicted person to which the justice assistant reports.

When the judicial decision pronouncing the autonomous probation sentence has acquired the force of res judicata, the clerk transmits within twenty-four hours a copy thereof to the president of the competent probation commission as well as to the competent district section of the Service of the Houses of Justice of the Federal Public Service Justice, which designates the justice assistant without delay.

Within the month following the designation of the justice assistant, and thereafter each time he deems it useful or each time the commission requests it, and at least every six months, he reports to the probation commission on compliance with the conditions. He proposes, where appropriate, the measures he deems useful.

###### § 2. The territorial jurisdiction of the probation commission is determined

by the place of residence of the convicted person at the time when the judgment or decision acquires the force of res judicata. Where the person concerned resides outside the territory of the Kingdom, the territorially competent probation commission is that of the place where the conviction was pronounced in first instance.

Where, in exceptional cases, the commission deems it appropriate, for a person sentenced to an autonomous probation sentence who makes a reasoned request to that effect, to transfer jurisdiction to the probation commission of the place of his new residence, it takes a reasoned decision, after this other commission has rendered a conforming opinion within a period of two months. For a person without residence in the Kingdom, jurisdiction may be transferred according to the same procedure to another probation commission, without it being required in this case that it be the commission of the place of his new residence.

###### § 3. The probation commission determines the concrete content of the autonomous

probation sentence, on the basis of the report of the justice assistant who has heard the convicted person and in compliance with the indications referred to in Article 37octies, § 4. The concrete content of the autonomous probation sentence is notified in an agreement to be signed by the convicted person, of which the justice assistant gives him a copy. The justice assistant also communicates a copy of the signed agreement to the probation commission, within a period of three working days.]1 (1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-10/80, art. 9, 111; Entry into force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13)>

Art. 37decies.[1 § 1er. The probation commission may suspend in whole or in part the concrete content of the autonomous probation sentence, specify it or adapt it to the circumstances, either of its own motion, or upon requisition of the public prosecutor, or at the request of the convicted person. In the case where one of the conditions of the autonomous probation sentence could not be fulfilled during the initial probation period without this being due to the will of the convicted person, the probation commission may extend the probation period once by a maximum of one year so that the convicted person can satisfy the condition.

If the probation commission considers that it must take one of the measures referred to in paragraph 1, the president convenes the interested party, by registered mail or by an electronic means to be defined by the King, more than ten days before the date set for the examination of the case. The file of the commission is made available for ten days to the interested party and his possible counsel.

If the probation commission considers that the autonomous probation sentence has been executed, it may decide that it ends, even if the period set by the judge has not yet expired.

The decision of the probation commission referred to in paragraph 1 or paragraph 3 is reasoned. This decision is notified to the interested party and to the public prosecutor. The notification is made to the public prosecutor by ordinary letter and to the interested party by registered mail or by an electronic means to be defined by the King, within three days, not including Saturdays, Sundays and public holidays.

###### § 2. The public prosecutor and the person convicted to the autonomous probation sentence

may, the first by requisition and the second by petition, bring before the court of first instance with which the commission is established, an appeal against the decisions taken by the commission by virtue of § 1er or by virtue of Article 37novies, § 3.

The requisition and the petition must be written and reasoned. The appeal must be brought within ten days of the notification of the decision of the commission. It is suspensive, unless the commission decides otherwise.

The president of the court called to rule has indicated more than ten days in advance, on a special register kept at the registry, the place, day and time of the appearance. The clerk gives notice thereof to the person convicted to the autonomous probation sentence by registered mail or by an electronic means to be defined by the King at least ten days before the appearance. During this period, the file is deposited at the registry and made available to the convicted person and his possible counsel. The court sits and rules in chambers.

If the court allows the appeal, it may reverse the decision of the commission. The decision rendered on this appeal is susceptible neither of appeal nor of opposition.]1 (1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-10/80, art. 10, 111; Entry into force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13)>>

Art. 37undecies.[1 In case of total or partial non-execution of the autonomous probation sentence, the justice assistant informs the probation commission without delay. The commission convenes the convicted person by registered mail or by an electronic means to be determined by the King more than ten days before the date set for the examination of the case and informs his counsel. The file of the commission is made available for five days to the convicted person and his possible counsel.

The commission, sitting in the absence of the public prosecutor, draws up a reasoned report with a view to the application of the substitute sentence.

The report is sent by ordinary letter to the convicted person, to the public prosecutor and to the justice assistant.

In this case, the public prosecutor may decide to execute the imprisonment or the fine provided for in the judicial decision, and this taking into account the autonomous probation sentence which has already been executed by the convicted person.]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-10/80, art. 11, 111; Entry into force: undetermined and at the latest on 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11- 23/02, art. 13)>>

###### SECTION VI. - SENTENCES COMMON TO THE THREE TYPES OF OFFENCE.

###### Sous-section I. - (Of the fine applicable to natural persons). <Inserted by L

1999-05-04/60, art. 7; Entry into force: 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 38. The fine for a petty offence is at least one [euro] and at most twenty-five

[euros], except in cases excepted by law. <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, Entry into force: 01-01-2002>

The fine for a crime or an offence is at least twenty-six [euros] <L 2000-06- 26/42, art. 2, Entry into force: 01-01-2002>.

The fines shall be collected for the benefit of the State.

###### Art. 39. The fine is pronounced individually against each of the persons convicted

in respect of the same offence.

###### Art. 40. In the absence of payment within two months from the judgment or

order, if it is adversarial, or from its service, if it is by default, the fine may be replaced by imprisonment the duration of which shall be fixed by the judgment or order of conviction, and which shall not exceed six months for persons convicted in respect of a crime, three months for persons convicted in respect of an offence, and three days for persons convicted in respect of a petty offence.

Convicted persons subject to subsidiary imprisonment may be detained in the establishment where they served the principal sentence.

If only a fine has been pronounced, the imprisonment to be served, in the absence of payment, is assimilated to correctional or police imprisonment, according to the nature of the conviction.

###### Art. 41. In all cases, the convicted person may free himself from this imprisonment

by paying the fine; he may not avoid proceedings against his property by offering to serve the imprisonment.

###### Sous-section II. - (Of the fine applicable to legal persons). <Inserted by L

1999-05-04/60, art. 8; Entry into force: 02-07-1999>

Art. 41bis. <Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art. 8; Entry into force: 02-07-1999> § 1er. The fines applicable to offences committed by legal persons are in criminal and correctional matters:

- where the law provides for the act a sentence of deprivation of liberty for life: a fine of two hundred and forty thousand [euros] to seven hundred and twenty thousand [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, Entry into force: 01-01-2002>;
- where the law provides for the act a sentence of deprivation of liberty and a fine, or one of these sentences only: a minimum fine of five hundred [euros] multiplied by the number of months corresponding to the minimum of the sentence of deprivation of liberty, and without being able to be less than the minimum of the fine provided for the act; the maximum amounts to two thousand [euros] multiplied by the number of months corresponding to the maximum of the sentence of deprivation of liberty, and without being able to be less than double the maximum of the fine provided for the act <L 2000-06- 26/42, art. 2, Entry into force: 01-01-2002>;
- where the law provides for the act only a fine: the minimum and the maximum are those provided by law for the act; in police matters:
- a fine of twenty-five [euros] to two hundred and fifty [euros] <L 2000-06- 26/42, art. 2, Entry into force: 01-01-2002>.

###### § 2. For the determination of the penalty provided for in § 1, the provisions of Book

I are applicable.

###### Subsection III. - (on special confiscation). <Inserted by L 1999-05-04/60, art.

9; In force: 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 42. Special confiscation applies:

1° To things forming the subject of the offence and to those which were used or intended to commit it, when ownership belongs to the convicted person; 2° To things that were produced by the offence.

(3° To the patrimonial advantages derived directly from the offence, to the goods and values that have been substituted for them and to the income from these invested advantages.) <L 1990-07-17/30, art. 1, 004; In force: 25-08-1990>

###### Art. 43. Special confiscation (applying to the things referred to in 1° and 2° of

article 42) shall always be pronounced for a crime or offence. [1 The confiscation of things which were used or intended to commit the crime or offence shall be ordered, except when it has the effect of subjecting the convicted person to an unreasonably heavy penalty.]1 <L 1990-07-17/30, art. 2, 004; In force: 25-08- It shall only be pronounced for a minor offence in the cases determined by law.

(1)<L 2018-03-18/14, art. 19, 129; In force: 12-05-2018>

Art. 43bis.<Inserted by L 1990-07-17/30, art. 3, 004; In force: 25-08-1990> (Special confiscation applying to the things referred to in article 42, 3°, may always be pronounced by the judge, but only to the extent that it is requested in writing by the public prosecutor.) <L 2002-12-19/86, art. 1, 039; In force: 24-02-2003>

[3 If the things provided for in paragraph 1 and the things which were used or intended to commit the offence cannot]3 be found in the estate of the convicted person, the judge shall proceed to their monetary valuation and the confiscation shall relate to a sum of money equivalent thereto.

When the confiscated things belong to the civil party, they shall be returned to them. The confiscated things shall likewise be attributed to them when the judge has pronounced their confiscation on the ground that they constitute goods or values substituted by the convicted person for things belonging to the civil party or because they constitute the equivalent of such things within the meaning of paragraph 2 of this article.

Any other third party claiming a right to the confiscated thing may assert that right within a period and according to procedures determined by the King.

[1 Special confiscation of immovable property must or may be pronounced by the judge, according to the applicable legal basis, but only to the extent that it has been requested in writing by the public prosecutor.

The written requisition of the public prosecutor for the confiscation of immovable property which has not been seized criminally in accordance with the applicable formalities is, on pain of inadmissibility, registered free of charge in the margin of the last transcribed title or of the judgment referred to in article 1, paragraphs 1 and 2, of the Mortgage Act of 16 December 1851. The public prosecutor attaches proof of the marginal note to the criminal file before the close of the proceedings. The public prosecutor requests, where appropriate, the free cancellation of the marginal note.]1 [2 The judge shall, if necessary, reduce the amount of the patrimonial advantages referred to in article 42, 3°, or the monetary valuation referred to in paragraph 2 in order not to subject the convicted person to an unreasonably heavy penalty.]2

(1)<L 2013-11-27/05, art. 2, 099; In force: 01-03-2014>

(2)<L 2014-02-11/12, art. 55, 100; In force: 18-04-2014>

(3)<L 2018-03-18/14, art. 20, 129; In force: 12-05-2018>

Art. 43ter. <Inserted by L 1997-05-20/50, art. 12, In force: 13-07-1997> Special confiscation applying to things referred to (in articles 42, 43bis and 43quater) may also be pronounced when these things are located outside the territory of Belgium. <L 2002-12-19/86, art. 3, 039; In force: 24-02- Art. 43quater.<inserted by L 2002-12-19/86, art. 4; In force: 24-02-2003> § 1. [2 Without prejudice to article 43bis, paragraphs 3 and 4, the patrimonial advantages referred to in paragraph 2, the goods and values that have been substituted for them and the income from the invested advantages found in the estate or in the possession of a person may, at the request of the public prosecutor, be confiscated or that person may be sentenced to pay an amount which the judge considers to correspond to the value of these things if they have been found guilty:

1° either of one or more offences referred to:

- a) in articles 136sexies and 136septies, 1°;
- b) in article 137, provided that these offences are punished by one of the penalties provided for in article 138, § 1, 4° to 10°, and that they are of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, in article 140, provided that this crime or offence is of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, in articles 140bis to 140sexies, provided that these offences are of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, in article 140septies, provided that this offence is punished by one of the penalties provided for in article 140septies, § 1, third and fourth indent, and that it is of a nature to generate patrimonial advantages, and in article
- c) in articles 162, 163, 173, 180 and 186;
- d) in articles 246 to 250;
- e) in articles 379 or 380 and 383bis;
- f) in articles 433quinquies to 433octies, 433undecies and 433duodecies;
- g) in articles 504bis and 504ter;
- h) in article 505, with the exception of things covered by article 42, 1°;
- i) in article 2bis, § 1, of the law of 24 February 1921 concerning the traffic of poisonous, soporific, narcotic, psychotropic, disinfecting or antiseptic substances and substances that may be used for the illicit manufacture of narcotic and psychotropic substances, provided that the facts relate to the import, export, manufacture, sale or offering for sale of the substances referred to in that article, or in article 2bis, § 3, b), or § 4, b);
- j) in article 2quater, 4°, of the same law;
- k) in articles 77bis to 77quinquies of the law of 15 December 1980 on access to the territory, residence, establishment and removal of aliens;
- l) in article 10, § 1, 2°, of the law of 15 July 1985 on the use of substances with hormonal, anti-hormonal, beta-adrenergic or production-stimulating effects in animals;

2° either of offences referred to in article 324ter;

3° either one or more offences referred to below, when they have been committed within the framework of a criminal organisation, as defined in article 324bis:

- a) in articles 468, 469, 470, 471 or 472;
- b) in article 475;
- c) in articles 477 to 477sexies or 488bis;
- d) in article 8 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the import, export, transit and control of trade in arms, ammunition and equipment specially designed for military use or for law enforcement purposes and related technology;
- e) in articles 1 and 8 of the royal decree of 12 April 1974 on certain operations concerning substances with hormonal, anti-hormonal, anabolic, beta-adrenergic, anti-infectious, antiparasitic and anti-inflammatory action, for offences punished in accordance with the law of 24 February 1921 concerning the traffic of poisonous, soporific, narcotic, psychotropic, disinfecting or antiseptic substances and substances that may be used for the illicit manufacture of narcotic and psychotropic substances;

4° either of several offences prosecuted collectively and whose seriousness, purpose and mutual relationship enable the court to decide certainly and necessarily that these acts were committed in the context of serious tax fraud, organised or not.]2

###### § 2. The confiscation referred to in § 1 may be pronounced against the perpetrators,

co-perpetrators and accomplices convicted of one or more of the offences listed in this article and under the conditions defined in § 1 if the convicted person has acquired during a relevant period additional patrimonial advantages while there are serious and concrete indications that these derive from the offence for which he was convicted or [2 from offences likely to give rise, directly or indirectly, to an economic advantage insofar as they appear under the same heading, provided for in

###### paragraph 1, as the offence which is the subject of the conviction]2 and that the

convicted person has not been able to make the contrary plausible.

This contrary may also be made plausible by any third party claiming to have a right to these advantages.

###### § 3. For the purposes of this article, the period shall be considered relevant

beginning five years before the person's indictment and running until the date of the judgment.

The serious and concrete indications referred to in § 2 may be drawn from all credible elements that have been submitted to the court in a regular manner and that show an imbalance of any interest between, on the one hand, the temporary or constant increase in the assets and expenditure of the convicted person during the relevant period, which the public prosecutor proves, and, on the other hand, the temporary or constant increase in the assets and expenditure of the convicted person during that period for which he can make it plausible that they do not derive from the facts for which he was convicted or from identical facts. When the court orders special confiscation within the meaning of this article, it may decide not to take into account part of the relevant period or income, property and values that it determines if it considers such a measure appropriate in order not to subject the convicted person to an unreasonably heavy sentence.

###### § 4.

The assets at the disposal of a criminal organization must be confiscated, subject to the rights of third parties in good faith.

(1)<L 2013-07-15/02, art. 14, 094; En vigueur : 29-07-2013>

(2)<L 2018-03-18/14, art. 21, 129; En vigueur : 12-05-2018>

##### CHAPITRE III. - OF OTHER CONVICTIONS THAT MAY BE PRONOUNCED

FOR CRIMES, MISDEMEANORS OR CONTRAVENTIONS.

###### Art. 44. Conviction to the penalties established by law shall always be pronounced

without prejudice to restitution and damages that may be owed to the parties.

###### Art. 45. When the law has not regulated damages, the court or

tribunal shall determine the amount, without however being able to pronounce its application to any work whatsoever, even with the consent of the injured party.

###### Art. 46.[1 The court or tribunal which finds guilty of one of the offences

referred to in articles 375, 398 to 400, 402, 403, 405, 409, §§ 1 to 3 and 5, and 422bis, a person liable to be called as a legal heir to the victim's succession, may also pronounce the testamentary unworthiness of the perpetrator, co-perpetrator or accomplice, who shall therefore be excluded from the victim's succession.]1 (1)<rétabli par L 2012-12-10/14, art. 42, 088; En vigueur : 21-01-2013>

###### Art. 47. (Repealed)

<L 31-01-1980, art. 4, 1°>

###### Art. 48. (Repealed)

<L 31-01-1980, art. 4, 1°>

###### Art. 49. When the assets of the convicted person are insufficient to cover the

convictions to the fine, restitution and damages, the last two convictions shall have preference.

In case of competition between the fine and the costs of justice owed to the State, payments made by the convicted persons shall be applied in the first place to these costs. (These payments interrupt the limitation period for both the fine and the costs of justice.) <L 2006-12-27/32, art. 302, 062; En vigueur : 07-01-2007>

###### Art. 50. All individuals convicted for the same offence are jointly and severally liable

for restitution and damages.

They are jointly and severally liable for costs when they have been convicted by the same judgment or ruling.

Nevertheless, the judge may exempt all or some of the convicted persons from joint and several liability, by indicating the reasons for this exemption, and by determining the proportion of costs to be borne individually by each of them.

Individuals convicted by separate judgments or rulings are only jointly and severally liable for costs in respect of acts of prosecution that were common to them.

Art. 50bis. <Inséré par L 1999-05-04/60, art. 10; En vigueur : 02-07-1999> No one may be held civilly liable for payment of a fine to which another person is sentenced, if he is convicted for the same facts.

##### CHAPITRE IV. - OF THE ATTEMPT TO COMMIT A CRIME OR MISDEMEANOR.

###### Art. 51. There is a punishable attempt when the resolution to commit a crime

or a misdemeanor has been manifested by external acts which form a commencement of execution of that crime or misdemeanor, and which have been suspended or have failed to have their effect only by circumstances independent of the will of the perpetrator.

###### Art. 52.The attempt to commit a crime is punished by the penalty immediately inferior to

that of the crime itself, in accordance with articles 80 and 81.

[1 Attempts at crimes punishable by life imprisonment or life detention shall however be punished respectively by imprisonment of twenty years to thirty years or by detention of twenty years to thirty years.]1 (1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 13, 114; En vigueur : 29-02-2016>

###### Art. 53. The law determines in which cases and by which penalties are punished

attempts to commit misdemeanors.

##### CHAPITRE V. - OF RECIDIVISM.

###### Art. 54. (Voir NOTE sous TITRE)

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 12, 040; En vigueur : 13- 03-2003> Whoever, having been convicted of a criminal penalty, commits a crime carrying imprisonment of five years to ten years, may be sentenced to imprisonment of ten years to fifteen years.

If the crime carries imprisonment of ten years to fifteen years, the offender may be sentenced to imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years.

He shall be sentenced to at least seventeen years of this penalty if the crime carries imprisonment of fifteen years to twenty years.

###### Art. 55. (Voir NOTE sous TITRE) Whoever, having been convicted of a criminal

penalty, commits a crime punished by detention of five years to ten years, may be sentenced to detention of ten years to fifteen years.

If the crime is punished by detention of ten years to fifteen years, the offender may be sentenced to (detention of fifteen years to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 13, 040; En vigueur : 13-03-2003>

He shall be sentenced to at least seventeen years of detention, if the crime carries (detention of fifteen years to twenty years). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 13, 040; En vigueur

###### Art. 56.Whoever, after a conviction to a criminal penalty, commits

a misdemeanor, may be sentenced to a penalty double the maximum provided by law against the misdemeanor.

The same penalty may be pronounced in case of a prior conviction to an imprisonment of at least one year, if the convicted person has committed the new misdemeanor before the expiration of five years from when he served or had his penalty prescribed. [1 Even in the cases referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2, if the new misdemeanor is a crime which has been correctionalized or for which the court of assizes has admitted the existence of mitigating circumstances, the duration of the imprisonment penalty may not exceed that of the maximum imprisonment penalty provided by law for that crime or forty years if said penalty is life imprisonment.]1

[1 In no case may the penalty pronounced exceed one year of electronic monitoring penalty, three hundred hours of work penalty or two years of autonomous probation penalty.]1

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 14, 114; En vigueur : 29-02-2016>

###### Art. 57. The rules established for recidivism shall be applied, in accordance with the

preceding articles, in case of a previous conviction pronounced by a military tribunal, for an act qualified as a felony or misdemeanor by the ordinary criminal laws, and to a penalty provided for by these same laws.

If, for this act, a penalty provided for by military laws has been pronounced, the courts and tribunals, in the assessment of recidivism, shall have regard only to the minimum of the penalty that the act punished by the first judgment could have entailed under the ordinary criminal laws.

Art. 57bis. [1 The rules established for recidivism, provided for in articles 54 to 56, are applied in case of a previous conviction taken into account in accordance with article 99bis.]1

(1)<Inserted by L 2014-04-25/23, art. 60, 104; In force: 24-05-2014>

##### CHAPTER VI. - CONCURRENCE OF SEVERAL OFFENCES.

###### Art. 58. Any individual convicted of several petty offences shall incur the penalty

for each of them.

(When community service sentences are pronounced, the duration thereof may be cumulated up to a maximum of three hundred hours.) <L 2002-04-17/33, art. 4, 035; In force: 07-05-2002>

[1 [2 When electronic monitoring sentences are pronounced, their duration may not exceed one year.]2 ]1

[2 When autonomous probation sentences are pronounced, their duration may not exceed two years.]2

01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02,

###### art. 13)

, art. 12 modified by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 52, 114>

###### Art. 59. In case of a concurrence of one or more misdemeanors with one or more

petty offences, [1 all fines, autonomous probation sentences, community service sentences, electronic monitoring sentences]1 and correctional imprisonment sentences shall be cumulated, within the limits set by the following article. <L 2002-04-17/33, art. 5, 035; In force: 07-05-2002> 01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02,

###### art. 13)

, art. 13, modified by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 53, 114>

###### Art. 60.

<L 01-02-1977, art. 5> In case of a concurrence of several misdemeanors, the penalties shall be cumulated without however being able to exceed double the maximum of the most severe penalty. [3 The sentence pronounced may not exceed either twenty years of imprisonment, or the most severe imprisonment sentence if this is greater than twenty years of imprisonment.]3 [2In no case may this sentence exceed [3 ...]3 one year of electronic monitoring sentence, three hundred hours of community service sentence or two years of autonomous probation sentence]2

01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself modified by L 2015-11-23/02,

###### art. 13)

, art. 14 modified by L 2016-02-05/11, art. 54, 114>

(3)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 15, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment annulled article 15) (See archived version no. 113)

###### Art. 61. When a felony is concurrent, either with one or more misdemeanors, or with one

or more petty offences, only the penalty for the felony shall be pronounced.

###### Art. 62. (See NOTE under TITLE) In case of a concurrence of several felonies, the most

severe penalty shall be pronounced alone. This penalty may even be increased by five years above the maximum, if it consists of (limited-term imprisonment or detention of fifteen to twenty years or a shorter term). <L 2003-01-23/42, art. 14, 040; In force: 13-03-2003>

###### Art. 63. (See NOTE under TITLE)

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 15, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> The most severe penalty is the one with the longest duration. If the penalties are of the same duration, imprisonment is considered a more severe penalty than detention.

###### Art. 64. Penalties of special confiscation, by reason of several felonies, misdemeanors

or petty offences, shall always be cumulated.

###### Art. 65.

<L 1994-07-11/33, art. 45, 012; In force: 31-07-1994> When the same act constitutes several offences or when different offences submitted simultaneously to the same trial judge constitute the successive and continuous manifestation of the same criminal intent, only the most severe penalty shall be pronounced.

When the trial judge finds that offences having previously been the subject of a final decision and other facts of which he is seized and which, assuming they are established, are prior to the said decision and constitute with the former the successive and continuous manifestation of the same criminal intent, he takes into account, for the determination of the penalty, the penalties already pronounced. If these appear to him to suffice for a just punishment of all the offences, he rules on the guilt and refers in his decision to the penalties already pronounced. The total of the penalties pronounced pursuant to this article may not exceed the maximum of the most severe penalty.

##### CHAPTER VII. - PARTICIPATION OF SEVERAL PERSONS IN THE SAME

FELONY OR MISDEMEANOR.

###### Art. 66. Shall be punished as perpetrators of a felony or misdemeanor:

Those who have executed it or who have directly cooperated in its execution;

Those who, by any act whatsoever, have lent such assistance to the execution that, without their assistance, the felony or misdemeanor could not have been committed;

Those who, by gifts, promises, threats, abuse of authority or power, machinations or culpable artifices, have directly incited to this felony or this misdemeanor;

(Those who, either by speeches made in meetings or in public places, or by writings, printed matter, images or emblems of any kind, which have been posted, distributed or sold, offered for sale or exposed to public view, have directly incited to commit it, without prejudice to the penalties provided by law against the perpetrators of incitements to felonies or misdemeanors, even in the case where such incitements have not been followed by effect.) <L 28-07-1934, art. 1, I>

###### Art. 67. Shall be punished as accomplices to a felony or misdemeanor:

Those who have given instructions to commit it;

Those who have procured weapons, instruments, or any other means which served for the felony or misdemeanor, knowing that they were to serve for it;

Those who, outside the case provided for by § 3 of article 66, have, with knowledge, aided or assisted the perpetrator or perpetrators of the felony or misdemeanor in the acts which prepared or facilitated it, or in those which consummated it.

###### Art. 68. Those who, knowing the criminal conduct of malefactors engaging in

brigandage or violence against the security of the State, public peace, persons or property, have habitually provided them with lodging, place of retreat or assembly, shall be punished as their accomplices.

###### Art. 69.Accomplices to a felony shall be punished with the penalty immediately

below that which they would incur if they were perpetrators of this felony, in accordance with articles 80 and 81 of this code. [1 They shall however be punished with imprisonment of twenty to thirty years or detention of twenty to thirty years if they were accomplices to a felony punishable by life imprisonment or life detention.]1

The penalty pronounced against accomplices to a misdemeanor shall not exceed two-thirds of that which would be applied to them if they were perpetrators of this misdemeanor. (1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 16, 114; In force: 29-02-2016>

##### CHAPTER VIII. - GROUNDS OF JUSTIFICATION AND EXCUSE.

###### Art. 70.

(Except with regard to the offences defined in book II, title Ibis, there is no offence), when the act was ordered by law and commanded by authority. <L 2003-08-05/32, art. 3, 044; In force: 07-08-2003>

###### Art. 71. [1 There is no offense when the accused or the defendant was suffering, at

the time of the facts, from a mental disorder that abolished [2 ...]2 their capacity for discernment or control of their acts or when they were compelled by a force which they could not resist.]1

(1)<L 2014-05-05/11, art. 87, 109; In force: 01-10-2016 (see art. 136).

###### Art. 78. No crime or offense may be excused, except in the cases

determined by law.

##### CHAPITRE IX. - MITIGATING CIRCUMSTANCES.

###### Art. 79. If mitigating circumstances exist, criminal penalties are

reduced or modified in accordance with the following provisions.

###### Art. 80.

<L 2001-12-11/50, art. 2, 033; In force: 17-02-2002> Life imprisonment shall be replaced by fixed-term imprisonment or by imprisonment of at least three years [1 and at most forty years]1.

[1 Imprisonment of thirty to forty years by imprisonment of thirty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least three years and at most thirty-eight years.

Imprisonment of twenty to thirty years, by imprisonment of twenty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least three years and at most twenty-eight years.]1

Imprisonment of fifteen to twenty years, by imprisonment of ten to fifteen years or five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least one year [1 and at most fifteen years]1.

Imprisonment of ten to fifteen years, by imprisonment of five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least six months [1 and at most ten years]1.

Imprisonment of five to ten years, by imprisonment of at least one month [1 and at most five years]1.

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 17, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment no. 148/2017 of 21-12-2017 (M.B. 12-01-2018, p. 1393), the Constitutional Court annulled article 17, 1° and 2° (elements in italics)

###### Art. 81.

<L 2003-01-23/42, art. 16, 040; In force: 13-03-2003> Life detention imposed for crimes against the external security of the State shall be replaced by fixed-term detention or by imprisonment of at least one year [1 and at most forty years]1.

[1 The penalty of detention of thirty to forty years by detention of thirty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least one year and at most thirty-eight years.

The penalty of detention of twenty to thirty years by detention of twenty-eight years or for a lesser term or by imprisonment of at least one year and at most twenty-eight years.]1

The penalty of detention of fifteen to twenty years by detention of ten to fifteen years or five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least one year [1 and at most fifteen years]1.

The penalty of detention of ten to fifteen years by detention of five to ten years or by imprisonment of at least six months [1 and at most ten years]1. The penalty of detention of five to ten years by imprisonment of at least one month [1 and at most five years]1.

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 18, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment no. 148/2017 of 21-12-2017 (M.B. 12-01-2018, p. 1393), the Constitutional Court annulled article 18, 1° and 2° (elements in italics))

###### Art. 82.

<L 23-08-1919, art. 2> In the cases of concurrence provided for in articles 61 and 62 of the Penal Code, if, by reason of mitigating circumstances, criminal penalties are reduced to the level of correctional penalties, the trial court may nevertheless impose only a single penalty.

###### Art. 83. The fine in criminal matters may be reduced, without it being able

to be in any case less than twenty-six [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>.

###### Art. 84.Offenders whose criminal penalty has been commuted to

imprisonment may be sentenced to a fine of twenty-six [euros] to one thousand [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01-2002>. (Paragraph 3 repealed) <L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

(1)<L 2009-12-21/14, art. 3, 075; In force: 21-01-2010>

(2)<L 2017-07-06/24, art. 209, 124; In force: 24-07-2017>

###### Art. 85.(If mitigating circumstances exist, [3 the penalties

of imprisonment, [4 ...]4 community service penalties, autonomous probation penalties and fine penalties may respectively be reduced below eight days, [4 ...]4 forty-five hours, twelve months and twenty-six euros]3, without them being able to be less than police penalties.) <L 2002-04-17/33, art. 7, 035; In force: 07-05-2002>

Judges may also apply one or the other of these penalties separately. If imprisonment is imposed alone, judges may substitute a fine that shall not exceed five hundred [euros] <L 2000-06-26/42, art. 2, In force: 01-01- If the prohibition of the rights enumerated in [1 article 31, paragraph 1]1 (...) is ordered or authorized, judges may impose these penalties for a term of one to five years, or remit them entirely. <L 09-04-1930, art. 32> (1)<L 2009-04-14/01, art. 7, 073; In force: 15-04-2009>

01-05-2016 (see L 2014-05-08/55, art. 6, itself amended by L 2015-11-23/02,

###### art. 13)

, art. 15 amended by L 2016-06-05/11, art. 55, 114> (4)<L 2018-07-11/02, art. 8, 131; In force: 28-07-2018>

##### CHAPITRE X. - EXTINCTION OF PENALTIES.

###### Art. 86. Penalties imposed by judgments that have become final

are extinguished by the death of the convicted person. (The loss of legal personality of the convicted legal person does not extinguish the penalty.) <L 1999-05-04/60, art. 11, 024; In force: 02-07-1999>

###### Art. 87. Incapacities imposed by judges or attached by law to

certain convictions cease by the remission that the King may grant, by virtue of the right of pardon.

###### Art. 88. (Repealed)

<L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

###### Art. 89. (Repealed)

<L 2004-11-22/32, art. 2, 049; In force: 19-12-2004> (NOTE: Confirmed by <L 2005-01-12/39, art. 169, 050; In force: 15-01-2007>)

###### Art. 90. (Repealed)

<L 2004-11-22/32, art. 2, 049; In force: 19-12-2004> (NOTE: Confirmed by <L 2005-01-12/39, art. 169, 050; In force: 15-01-2007>)

###### Art. 91. (Except for penalties concerning the offenses defined in articles

136bis, 136ter and 136quater, criminal penalties shall be prescribed) by twenty complete years, from the date of the judgments imposing them. <L 2003-08-05/32, art. , 044; In force: 07-08-2003>

###### Art. 92.[1 Except for penalties concerning the offenses defined in articles

136bis, 136ter and 136quater, which are imprescriptible, correctional penalties shall be prescribed by five complete years]1, from the date of the judgment rendered in final resort, or from the day when the judgment rendered in first instance can no longer be challenged by appeal.

If the penalty imposed exceeds three years, the prescription shall be ten years. [1 If the penalty of imprisonment imposed exceeds twenty years, the prescription shall be twenty years.]1

(1)<L 2016-02-05/11, art. 19, 114; In force: 29-02-2016> (NOTE: by its judgment no. 148/2017 of 21-12-2017 (M.B. 12-01-2018, p. 1393), the Constitutional Court annulled article 19, 2° (elements in italics))

###### Art. 93. Police penalties shall be prescribed by one complete year, from the

periods fixed in the preceding article.

###### Art. 94.[1 Fines shall be subject to limitation within the time limits fixed by the

preceding articles, according to whether they are imposed for crimes, misdemeanors or petty offenses.

Special confiscations shall be subject to limitation within the time limits fixed by the preceding articles, according to whether they are imposed for petty offenses or crimes. Special confiscations imposed for misdemeanors shall be subject to limitation after ten full years, from the times determined in Article 92.]1 (1)<L 2014-02-11/12, art. 48, 100; En vigueur : 18-04-2014>

###### Art. 95. If the convicted person who was serving his sentence has managed to escape, the

limitation period begins to run from the day of escape.

However, in this case, the time during which the convicted person served his sentence beyond five years, if it is a temporary criminal penalty, or beyond two years, if it is a correctional penalty, shall be credited against the duration of the limitation period.

###### Art. 96. The limitation period for the penalty shall be interrupted by the arrest of the

convicted person.

###### Art. 97.

[1 § 1er. The limitation period for confiscation is suspended when the law so provides or when there is a legal impediment to the immediate execution of this penalty.

###### § 2. The limitation period is in any case suspended in the following cases:

1° while the convicted person is subject to collective legal insolvency proceedings;

2° during the processing of the petition for pardon concerning the confiscation incurred, filed by the convicted person or third parties in accordance with Articles 110 and 111 of the Constitution;

3° during the duration of a settlement plan granted to the convicted person by the competent official of the Federal Public Service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation, fine or legal costs.]1 (1)<rétabli par L 2014-02-11/12, art. 49, 100; En vigueur : 18-04-2014>

###### Art. 98.

[1 § 1er. The limitation period for confiscation is interrupted by any act of execution emanating from the legally competent authorities.

###### § 2. The limitation period is in any case interrupted in the following cases:

1° any partial payment made by or for the convicted person to the competent official of the Federal Public Service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation that does not fall within the framework of a settlement plan granted by the receiver;

2° any request for payment or any formal notice addressed to the convicted person, by registered mail or by bailiff's writ, and emanating from the competent official of the Federal Public Service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation;

3° any seizure carried out by or at the request of the competent official of the Federal Public Service Finance responsible for the recovery of the confiscation; 4° the decision of the director of the Central Body for Seizure and Confiscation to investigate the solvency of the convicted person;

5° the decision of the public prosecutor's office to open a criminal enforcement investigation in accordance with Article 464/1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure;

6° all acts of execution performed within the framework of the criminal enforcement investigation in accordance with Article 464/1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure.]1 (1)<L 2014-02-11/12, art. 50, 100; En vigueur : 18-04-2014>

###### Art. 99.Civil convictions, pronounced by judgments or decisions rendered in criminal,

correctional or police matters, shall be subject to limitation according to the rules of civil law, from the day they become irrevocable. [1 Succession unworthiness, pronounced by the judge on the basis of Article 46, is imprescriptible. It may be lifted by pardon, granted by the victim in accordance with Article 728 of the Civil Code.]1

(1)<rétabli par L 2012-12-10/14, art. 43, 088; En vigueur : 21-01-2013>

##### CHAPTER XI. [1 - Taking into account convictions pronounced by the criminal

courts of other States]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2014-04-25/23, art. 61, 104; En vigueur : 24-05-2014> Art. 99bis. [1 Convictions pronounced by the criminal courts of another Member State of the European Union are taken into account under the same conditions as convictions pronounced by Belgian criminal courts, and they shall produce the same legal effects as these convictions. The rule mentioned in paragraph 1 is not applicable to the case referred to in Article 65, paragraph 2.]1

(1)<Inséré par L 2014-04-25/23, art. 62, 104; En vigueur : 24-05-2014> (GENERAL PROVISIONS.) <L 2000-11-28/35, art. 2; En vigueur : 27-03-2001>

###### Art. 100. (In the absence of contrary provisions in special laws and regulations,

the provisions of Book One of this Code shall be applied to offenses provided for by these laws and regulations, with the exception of Chapter VII (...), and Article 85.) <L 09-04-1930, art. 32>

(Paragraph 2 repealed) <L 04-08-1986, art. 105>

Art. 100bis. <L 28-07-1934, art. 1, II> They are applied without exception to persons who, not being subject to military criminal laws, have participated in a crime or misdemeanor punished by the Military Criminal Code. However, military imprisonment is replaced by imprisonment of the same duration and dismissal, imposed as a principal penalty, by imprisonment from two months to three years.

Art. 100ter. <Inséré par L 2000-11-28/35, art. 3, 029; En vigueur : 27-03-2001> When the term "minor" is used in the provisions of Book II, this notion designates the person who has not yet reached the age of eighteen years.

## BOOK 2. - OFFENSES AND THEIR PUNISHMENT IN PARTICULAR.

#### TITLE I. - CRIMES AND MISDEMEANORS AGAINST STATE SECURITY.

##### CHAPTER I. - ATTACKS AND CONSPIRACIES AGAINST THE KING, AGAINST THE

ROYAL FAMILY AND AGAINST THE FORM OF GOVERNMENT.

###### Art. 101. An attack on the life or person of the King shall be punished (by life
